headline: Kimberly-Clark vs. The Clorox: Which Consumer Goods Stock Is a Better Buy in 2026?
<<<SUMMARY>>>
The article compares Kimberly-Clark and Clorox as investment options for 2026. Both companies face challenges including high debt loads, customer concentration risk (Walmart accounts for 16-27% of sales), and intense competition. Kimberly-Clark is undergoing significant restructuring with a potential $48 billion merger with Kenvue and selling its international tissue business, while Clorox is recovering from a 2023 cyberattack and pandemic-era slowdown. The author recommends Clorox as the safer choice due to its stronger brand positioning, though acknowledges Kimberly-Clark offers higher upside potential despite greater integration risks.
<<<INSIGHTS>>>
KMB | neutral | Company shows mixed signals with declining revenue ($16.4B vs $16.8B) and net income ($2.0B vs $2.5B), but maintains reasonable valuation (Forward P/E 13.2x). However, significant integration risks from pending $48 billion Kenvue merger and international tissue business sale create uncertainty. Author suggests waiting for clarity before investing.
CLX | positive | Despite flat revenue ($7.1B), net income improved significantly ($810M vs $280M), indicating margin recovery post-cyberattack. Trading at P/S ratio of 1.6x well below 10-year average of 2.9x suggests undervaluation. Strong brand positioning with 80% of sales from No. 1 or No. 2 market share products. Author recommends as the safer choice for 2026.
KVUE | neutral | Mentioned as target of Kimberly-Clark's potential $48 billion merger. While the combined entity could create a personal care juggernaut, significant debt and integration risks exist. Motley Fool has positions and recommends the stock, but article emphasizes uncertainty around deal completion and execution.
WMT | neutral | Mentioned as major customer concentration risk for both KMB (16% of sales) and CLX (27% of sales), giving retailers significant pricing power. Motley Fool recommends the stock, but article highlights this as a risk factor for the consumer goods companies analyzed.

North American category growth slowed to 1.9% in Q2 2026 amid consumer pressures, while the company achieved notable market-share gains in international markets like Indonesia (390 basis points in diapers) and Brazil (70 basis points). However, Zacks rates KMB as a Sell with shares down 7.7% in three months, and consensus estimates project minimal earnings growth ahead.
<<<INSIGHTS>>>
KMB | negative | Despite operational resilience and market-share gains, KMB faces headwinds from moderating category growth (2.5% to 2%), slowing North American growth (3.7% to 1.9%), and weak stock performance (-7.7% in 3 months). Zacks rates it as Rank #4 (Sell) with minimal earnings growth projections (2.1% next year), indicating limited upside potential.
NWL | positive | NWL carries a Zacks Rank #1 (Strong Buy) with strong consensus estimates projecting 33.3% earnings growth and a trailing four-quarter average earnings surprise of nearly 40%, indicating consistent outperformance.
PRPL | positive | PRPL holds a Zacks Rank #2 (Buy) with consensus estimates projecting 23.5% earnings growth despite a slight 8.5% sales decline, and demonstrates a solid 21.3% average trailing four-quarter earnings surprise.
RYKKY | positive | RYKKY carries a Zacks Rank #2 (Buy) with consensus estimates projecting healthy growth of 9.5% in sales and 16.7% in earnings, indicating solid fundamental performance.
